load_model(\u0026quot;bnlearn/alarm\u0026quot;)\nalarm_df = discrete_bn.simulate(n_samples=100)\n\n# Learn a network from simulated data.\nfrom pgmpy.estimators import PC\n\ndag = PC(data=alarm_df).estimate(ci_test=\u0026quot;chi_square\u0026quot;, return_type=\u0026quot;dag\u0026quot;)\n\n# Learn the parameters from the data.\nfrom pgmpy.models import DiscreteBayesianNetwork\n\ndiscrete_bn = DiscreteBayesianNetwork(dag.edges())\ndiscrete_bn.add_nodes_from(dag.nodes())\ndag_fitted = discrete_bn.fit(alarm_df)\ndag_fitted.get_cpds()\n\n# Drop a column data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"from pgmpy.example_models import load_model\n\n# Load an example Gaussian Bayesian Network and simulate data\ngaussian_bn = load_model(\u0026quot;bnlearn/ecoli70\u0026quot;)\necoli_df = gaussian_bn.simulate(n_samples=100)\n\n# Learn the network from simulated data.\nfrom pgmpy.estimators import PC\n\ndag = PC(data=ecoli_df).estimate(ci_test=\u0026quot;pearsonr\u0026quot;, return_type=\u0026quot;dag\u0026quot;)\n\n# Learn the parameters from the data.\nfrom pgmpy.models import dir=\"auto\"\u003eMixture Data with Arbitrary Relationships\u003c/h4\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-mixture-data-with-arbitrary-relationships\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Mixture Data width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"from pgmpy.global_vars import config\n\nconfig.set_backend(\u0026quot;torch\u0026quot;)\n\nimport pyro.distributions as dist\n\nfrom pgmpy.models import FunctionalBayesianNetwork\nfrom pgmpy.factors.hybrid import FunctionalCPD\n\n# Create a Bayesian Network with mixture of discrete and continuous variables.\nfunc_bn = FunctionalBayesianNetwork(\n    [\n        (\u0026quot;x1\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;w\u0026quot;),\n        (\u0026quot;x2\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;w\u0026quot;),\n        (\u0026quot;x1\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;y\u0026quot;),\n        (\u0026quot;x2\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;y\u0026quot;),\n        (\u0026quot;w\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;y\u0026quot;),\n        (\u0026quot;y\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;z\u0026quot;),\n        (\u0026quot;w\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;z\u0026quot;),\n        (\u0026quot;y\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;c\u0026quot;),\n        (\u0026quot;w\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;c\u0026quot;),\n    ]\n)\n\n# Define the Functional CPDs for each node and add them to the model.\ncpd_x1 = FunctionalCPD(\u0026quot;x1\u0026quot;, fn=lambda _: dist.Normal(0.0, 1.0))\ncpd_x2 = FunctionalCPD(\u0026quot;x2\u0026quot;, fn=lambda _: dist.Normal(0.5, 1.2))\n\n# Continuous mediator: w = 0.7*x1 - 0.3*x2 + ε\ncpd_w = FunctionalCPD(\n    \u0026quot;w\u0026quot;,\n    fn=lambda parents: dist.Normal(0.7 * parents[\u0026quot;x1\u0026quot;] - 0.3 * parents[\u0026quot;x2\u0026quot;], 0.5),\n    parents=[\u0026quot;x1\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;x2\u0026quot;],\n)\n\n# Bernoulli target with logistic link: y ~ Bernoulli(sigmoid(-0.7 + 1.5*x1 + 0.8*x2 + 1.2*w))\ncpd_y = FunctionalCPD(\n    \u0026quot;y\u0026quot;,\n    fn=lambda parents: dist.Bernoulli(\n        logits=(-0.7 + 1.5 * parents[\u0026quot;x1\u0026quot;] + 0.8 * parents[\u0026quot;x2\u0026quot;] + 1.2 * parents[\u0026quot;w\u0026quot;])\n    ),\n    parents=[\u0026quot;x1\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;x2\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;w\u0026quot;],\n)\n\n# Downstream Bernoulli influenced by y and w\ncpd_z = FunctionalCPD(\n    \u0026quot;z\u0026quot;,\n    fn=lambda parents: dist.Bernoulli(\n        logits=(-1.2 + 0.8 * parents[\u0026quot;y\u0026quot;] + 0.2 * parents[\u0026quot;w\u0026quot;])\n    ),\n    parents=[\u0026quot;y\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;w\u0026quot;],\n)\n\n# Continuous outcome depending on y and w: c = 0.2 + 0.5*y + 0.3*w + ε\ncpd_c = FunctionalCPD(\n    \u0026quot;c\u0026quot;,\n    fn=lambda parents: dist.Normal(0.2 + 0.5 * parents[\u0026quot;y\u0026quot;] + 0.3 * parents[\u0026quot;w\u0026quot;], 0.7),\n    parents=[\u0026quot;y\u0026quot;, \u0026quot;w\u0026quot;],\n)\n\nfunc_bn.add_cpds(cpd_x1, cpd_x2, cpd_w, cpd_y, cpd_z, cpd_c)\nfunc_bn.check_model()\n\n# Simulate data from the model\ndf_func = func_bn.simulate(n_samples=1000, seed=123)\n\n# For learning and inference in Functional Bayesian Networks, please refer to the example notebook: https://github.com/pgmpy/pgmpy/blob/dev/examples/Functional_Bayesian_Network_Tutorial.ipynb\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003efrom\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan   (\u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\"x2\"\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\"w\"\u003c/span\u003e),\n        (\u003cspan class=\"pl-s\"\u003e\"x1\"\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan \u003cspan class=\"pl-s1\"\u003e_\u003c/span\u003e: \u003cspan class=\"pl-s1\"\u003edist\u003c/span\u003e.\u003cspan class=\"pl-c1\"\u003eNormal\u003c/span\u003e(\u003cspan class="js-snippet-clipboard-copy-unpositioned SharedMarkdownContent__JRRdYK" data-hpc="true" containertiming="hpc"><article class="markdown-body entry-content container-lg" itemprop="text"><div dir="auto">
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://www.pgmpy.org" rel="nofollow"><img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pgmpy/pgmpy/dev/logo/logo_color.png" width="175" align="left" style="max-width: 100%;"></a>
pgmpy provides the building blocks for causal and probabilistic reasoning using graphical models. It implements data structures for a range of causal and graphical models such as DAGs, PDAGs, MAGs, PAGs, Bayesian Networks, Dynamic Bayesian Networks, and Structural Equation Models, along with algorithms for various tasks such as causal discovery, causal identification, causal and probabilistic inference, model validation, parameter estimation, simulations, and more.</p>
<p dir="auto">Algorithms for each task follow a unified composable API, making them modular and extensible. They are also scikit-learn compatible when possible. They can be used directly, combined in sklearn pipelines, or used to build higher-level tools on top of them.</p>
</div>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Key Features</h2><a id="user-content-key-features" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Key Features" href="#key-features"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Feature</th>
<th>Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://pgmpy.org/guides/causal_discovery.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Causal Discovery / Structure Learning</strong></a></td>
<td>Learn the model structure from data, with optional integration of <strong>expert knowledge</strong>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://pgmpy.org/guides/metrics.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Causal Validation</strong></a></td>
<td>Assess how compatible the causal structure is with the data.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://pgmpy.org/guides/parameter_estimation.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Parameter Learning</strong></a></td>
<td>Estimate model parameters (e.g., conditional probability distributions) from observed data.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://pgmpy.org/guides/probabilistic_inference.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Probabilistic Inference</strong></a></td>
<td>Compute posterior distributions conditioned on observed evidence.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://pgmpy.org/api/causal_inference.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Causal Inference</strong></a></td>
<td>Compute interventional distributions.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://pgmpy.org/guides/simulations.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Simulations</strong></a></td>
<td>Generate synthetic data under specified evidence or interventions.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>Example</strong> <a href="https://pgmpy.org/guides/datasets.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Datasets</strong></a> <strong>and</strong> <a href="https://pgmpy.org/guides/example_models.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Models</strong></a></td>
<td>Collection of datasets and models from various sources.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://pgmpy.org/guides/plotting.html" rel="nofollow"><strong>Plotting</strong></a></td>
<td>Flexible plotting functionality.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Quickstart</h2><a id="user-content-quickstart" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Quickstart" href="#quickstart"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Installation</h3><a id="user-content-installation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Installation" href="#installation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">pgmpy is available on both <a href="https://pypi.org/project/pgmpy/" rel="nofollow">PyPI</a> and <a href="https://anaconda.org/conda-forge/pgmpy" rel="nofollow">anaconda</a>. To install from PyPI, use:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="pip install pgmpy"><pre>pip install pgmpy</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">To install from conda-forge, use:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="conda install conda-forge::pgmpy"><pre>conda install conda-forge::pgmpy</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Examples</h3><a id="user-content-examples" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Examples" href="#examples"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h4 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Discrete Data</h4><a id="user-content-discrete-data" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Discrete Data" href="#discrete-data"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from pgmpy.example_models import load_model

# Load a Discrete Bayesian Network and simulate data.
discrete_bn = load_model(&quot;bnlearn/alarm&quot;)
alarm_df = discrete_bn.simulate(n_samples=100)

# Learn a network from simulated data.
from pgmpy.estimators import PC

dag = PC(data=alarm_df).estimate(ci_test=&quot;chi_square&quot;, return_type=&quot;dag&quot;)

# Learn the parameters from the data.
from pgmpy.models import DiscreteBayesianNetwork

discrete_bn = DiscreteBayesianNetwork(dag.edges())
discrete_bn.add_nodes_from(dag.nodes())
dag_fitted = discrete_bn.fit(alarm_df)
dag_fitted.get_cpds()

# Drop a column and predict using the learned model.
evidence_df = alarm_df.drop(columns=[&quot;FIO2&quot;], axis=1)
pred_FIO2 = dag_fitted.predict(evidence_df)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">example_models</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">load_model</span>

<span class="pl-c"># Load a Discrete Bayesian Network and simulate data.</span>
<span class="pl-s1">discrete_bn</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">load_model</span>(<span class="pl-s">"bnlearn/alarm"</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">alarm_df</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">discrete_bn</span>.<span class="pl-c1">simulate</span>(<span class="pl-s1">n_samples</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">100</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># Learn a network from simulated data.</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">estimators</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-c1">PC</span>

<span class="pl-s1">dag</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">PC</span>(<span class="pl-s1">data</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">alarm_df</span>).<span class="pl-c1">estimate</span>(<span class="pl-s1">ci_test</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"chi_square"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">return_type</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"dag"</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># Learn the parameters from the data.</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">models</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">DiscreteBayesianNetwork</span>

<span class="pl-s1">discrete_bn</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">DiscreteBayesianNetwork</span>(<span class="pl-s1">dag</span>.<span class="pl-c1">edges</span>())
<span class="pl-s1">discrete_bn</span>.<span class="pl-c1">add_nodes_from</span>(<span class="pl-s1">dag</span>.<span class="pl-c1">nodes</span>())
<span class="pl-s1">dag_fitted</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">discrete_bn</span>.<span class="pl-c1">fit</span>(<span class="pl-s1">alarm_df</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">dag_fitted</span>.<span class="pl-c1">get_cpds</span>()

<span class="pl-c"># Drop a column and predict using the learned model.</span>
<span class="pl-s1">evidence_df</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">alarm_df</span>.<span class="pl-c1">drop</span>(<span class="pl-s1">columns</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s">"FIO2"</span>], <span class="pl-s1">axis</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">1</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">pred_FIO2</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">dag_fitted</span>.<span class="pl-c1">predict</span>(<span class="pl-s1">evidence_df</span>)</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h4 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Linear Gaussian Data</h4><a id="user-content-linear-gaussian-data" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Linear Gaussian Data" href="#linear-gaussian-data"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from pgmpy.example_models import load_model

# Load an example Gaussian Bayesian Network and simulate data
gaussian_bn = load_model(&quot;bnlearn/ecoli70&quot;)
ecoli_df = gaussian_bn.simulate(n_samples=100)

# Learn the network from simulated data.
from pgmpy.estimators import PC

dag = PC(data=ecoli_df).estimate(ci_test=&quot;pearsonr&quot;, return_type=&quot;dag&quot;)

# Learn the parameters from the data.
from pgmpy.models import LinearGaussianBayesianNetwork

gaussian_bn = LinearGaussianBayesianNetwork(dag.edges())
dag_fitted = gaussian_bn.fit(ecoli_df)
dag_fitted.get_cpds()

# Drop a column and predict using the learned model.
evidence_df = ecoli_df.drop(columns=[&quot;ftsJ&quot;], axis=1)
pred_ftsJ = dag_fitted.predict(evidence_df)"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">example_models</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">load_model</span>

<span class="pl-c"># Load an example Gaussian Bayesian Network and simulate data</span>
<span class="pl-s1">gaussian_bn</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">load_model</span>(<span class="pl-s">"bnlearn/ecoli70"</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">ecoli_df</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">gaussian_bn</span>.<span class="pl-c1">simulate</span>(<span class="pl-s1">n_samples</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">100</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># Learn the network from simulated data.</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">estimators</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-c1">PC</span>

<span class="pl-s1">dag</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">PC</span>(<span class="pl-s1">data</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s1">ecoli_df</span>).<span class="pl-c1">estimate</span>(<span class="pl-s1">ci_test</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"pearsonr"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">return_type</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-s">"dag"</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># Learn the parameters from the data.</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">models</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">LinearGaussianBayesianNetwork</span>

<span class="pl-s1">gaussian_bn</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">LinearGaussianBayesianNetwork</span>(<span class="pl-s1">dag</span>.<span class="pl-c1">edges</span>())
<span class="pl-s1">dag_fitted</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">gaussian_bn</span>.<span class="pl-c1">fit</span>(<span class="pl-s1">ecoli_df</span>)

<span class="pl-c"># Drop a column and predict using the learned model.</span>
<span class="pl-s1">evidence_df</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">ecoli_df</span>.<span class="pl-c1">drop</span>(<span class="pl-s1">columns</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s">"ftsJ"</span>], <span class="pl-s1">axis</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-c1">1</span>)
<span class="pl-s1">pred_ftsJ</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">dag_fitted</span>.<span class="pl-c1">predict</span>(<span class="pl-s1">evidence_df</span>)</pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h4 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Mixture Data with Arbitrary Relationships</h4><a id="user-content-mixture-data-with-arbitrary-relationships" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Mixture Data with Arbitrary Relationships" href="#mixture-data-with-arbitrary-relationships"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-python notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="from pgmpy.global_vars import config

config.set_backend(&quot;torch&quot;)

import pyro.distributions as dist

from pgmpy.models import FunctionalBayesianNetwork
from pgmpy.factors.hybrid import FunctionalCPD

# Create a Bayesian Network with mixture of discrete and continuous variables.
func_bn = FunctionalBayesianNetwork(
    [
        (&quot;x1&quot;, &quot;w&quot;),
        (&quot;x2&quot;, &quot;w&quot;),
        (&quot;x1&quot;, &quot;y&quot;),
        (&quot;x2&quot;, &quot;y&quot;),
        (&quot;w&quot;, &quot;y&quot;),
        (&quot;y&quot;, &quot;z&quot;),
        (&quot;w&quot;, &quot;z&quot;),
        (&quot;y&quot;, &quot;c&quot;),
        (&quot;w&quot;, &quot;c&quot;),
    ]
)

# Define the Functional CPDs for each node and add them to the model.
cpd_x1 = FunctionalCPD(&quot;x1&quot;, fn=lambda _: dist.Normal(0.0, 1.0))
cpd_x2 = FunctionalCPD(&quot;x2&quot;, fn=lambda _: dist.Normal(0.5, 1.2))

# Continuous mediator: w = 0.7*x1 - 0.3*x2 + ε
cpd_w = FunctionalCPD(
    &quot;w&quot;,
    fn=lambda parents: dist.Normal(0.7 * parents[&quot;x1&quot;] - 0.3 * parents[&quot;x2&quot;], 0.5),
    parents=[&quot;x1&quot;, &quot;x2&quot;],
)

# Bernoulli target with logistic link: y ~ Bernoulli(sigmoid(-0.7 + 1.5*x1 + 0.8*x2 + 1.2*w))
cpd_y = FunctionalCPD(
    &quot;y&quot;,
    fn=lambda parents: dist.Bernoulli(
        logits=(-0.7 + 1.5 * parents[&quot;x1&quot;] + 0.8 * parents[&quot;x2&quot;] + 1.2 * parents[&quot;w&quot;])
    ),
    parents=[&quot;x1&quot;, &quot;x2&quot;, &quot;w&quot;],
)

# Downstream Bernoulli influenced by y and w
cpd_z = FunctionalCPD(
    &quot;z&quot;,
    fn=lambda parents: dist.Bernoulli(
        logits=(-1.2 + 0.8 * parents[&quot;y&quot;] + 0.2 * parents[&quot;w&quot;])
    ),
    parents=[&quot;y&quot;, &quot;w&quot;],
)

# Continuous outcome depending on y and w: c = 0.2 + 0.5*y + 0.3*w + ε
cpd_c = FunctionalCPD(
    &quot;c&quot;,
    fn=lambda parents: dist.Normal(0.2 + 0.5 * parents[&quot;y&quot;] + 0.3 * parents[&quot;w&quot;], 0.7),
    parents=[&quot;y&quot;, &quot;w&quot;],
)

func_bn.add_cpds(cpd_x1, cpd_x2, cpd_w, cpd_y, cpd_z, cpd_c)
func_bn.check_model()

# Simulate data from the model
df_func = func_bn.simulate(n_samples=1000, seed=123)

# For learning and inference in Functional Bayesian Networks, please refer to the example notebook: https://github.com/pgmpy/pgmpy/blob/dev/examples/Functional_Bayesian_Network_Tutorial.ipynb"><pre><span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">global_vars</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">config</span>

<span class="pl-s1">config</span>.<span class="pl-c1">set_backend</span>(<span class="pl-s">"torch"</span>)

<span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-s1">pyro</span>.<span class="pl-s1">distributions</span> <span class="pl-k">as</span> <span class="pl-s1">dist</span>

<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">models</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">FunctionalBayesianNetwork</span>
<span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s1">pgmpy</span>.<span class="pl-s1">factors</span>.<span class="pl-s1">hybrid</span> <span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-v">FunctionalCPD</span>

<span class="pl-c"># Create a Bayesian Network with mixture of discrete and continuous variables.</span>
<span class="pl-s1">func_bn</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">FunctionalBayesianNetwork</span>(
    [
        (<span class="pl-s">"x1"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"w"</span>),
        (<span class="pl-s">"x2"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"w"</span>),
        (<span class="pl-s">"x1"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"y"</span>),
        (<span class="pl-s">"x2"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"y"</span>),
        (<span class="pl-s">"w"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"y"</span>),
        (<span class="pl-s">"y"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"z"</span>),
        (<span class="pl-s">"w"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"z"</span>),
        (<span class="pl-s">"y"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"c"</span>),
        (<span class="pl-s">"w"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"c"</span>),
    ]
)

<span class="pl-c"># Define the Functional CPDs for each node and add them to the model.</span>
<span class="pl-s1">cpd_x1</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">FunctionalCPD</span>(<span class="pl-s">"x1"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">fn</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-k">lambda</span> <span class="pl-s1">_</span>: <span class="pl-s1">dist</span>.<span class="pl-c1">Normal</span>(<span class="pl-c1">0.0</span>, <span class="pl-c1">1.0</span>))
<span class="pl-s1">cpd_x2</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">FunctionalCPD</span>(<span class="pl-s">"x2"</span>, <span class="pl-s1">fn</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-k">lambda</span> <span class="pl-s1">_</span>: <span class="pl-s1">dist</span>.<span class="pl-c1">Normal</span>(<span class="pl-c1">0.5</span>, <span class="pl-c1">1.2</span>))

<span class="pl-c"># Continuous mediator: w = 0.7*x1 - 0.3*x2 + ε</span>
<span class="pl-s1">cpd_w</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">FunctionalCPD</span>(
    <span class="pl-s">"w"</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">fn</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-k">lambda</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>: <span class="pl-s1">dist</span>.<span class="pl-c1">Normal</span>(<span class="pl-c1">0.7</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"x1"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">-</span> <span class="pl-c1">0.3</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"x2"</span>], <span class="pl-c1">0.5</span>),
    <span class="pl-s1">parents</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s">"x1"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"x2"</span>],
)

<span class="pl-c"># Bernoulli target with logistic link: y ~ Bernoulli(sigmoid(-0.7 + 1.5*x1 + 0.8*x2 + 1.2*w))</span>
<span class="pl-s1">cpd_y</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">FunctionalCPD</span>(
    <span class="pl-s">"y"</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">fn</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-k">lambda</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>: <span class="pl-s1">dist</span>.<span class="pl-c1">Bernoulli</span>(
        <span class="pl-s1">logits</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>(<span class="pl-c1">-</span><span class="pl-c1">0.7</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-c1">1.5</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"x1"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-c1">0.8</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"x2"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-c1">1.2</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"w"</span>])
    ),
    <span class="pl-s1">parents</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s">"x1"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"x2"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"w"</span>],
)

<span class="pl-c"># Downstream Bernoulli influenced by y and w</span>
<span class="pl-s1">cpd_z</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">FunctionalCPD</span>(
    <span class="pl-s">"z"</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">fn</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-k">lambda</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>: <span class="pl-s1">dist</span>.<span class="pl-c1">Bernoulli</span>(
        <span class="pl-s1">logits</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>(<span class="pl-c1">-</span><span class="pl-c1">1.2</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-c1">0.8</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"y"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-c1">0.2</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"w"</span>])
    ),
    <span class="pl-s1">parents</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s">"y"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"w"</span>],
)

<span class="pl-c"># Continuous outcome depending on y and w: c = 0.2 + 0.5*y + 0.3*w + ε</span>
<span class="pl-s1">cpd_c</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">FunctionalCPD</span>(
    <span class="pl-s">"c"</span>,
    <span class="pl-s1">fn</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span><span class="pl-k">lambda</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>: <span class="pl-s1">dist</span>.<span class="pl-c1">Normal</span>(<span class="pl-c1">0.2</span> <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-c1">0.5</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"y"</span>] <span class="pl-c1">+</span> <span class="pl-c1">0.3</span> <span class="pl-c1">*</span> <span class="pl-s1">parents</span>[<span class="pl-s">"w"</span>], <span class="pl-c1">0.7</span>),
    <span class="pl-s1">parents</span><span class="pl-c1">=</span>[<span class="pl-s">"y"</span>, <span class="pl-s">"w"</span>],
)

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Contributing</h2><a id="user-content-contributing" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Contributing" href="#contributing"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">We welcome all contributions --not just code-- to pgmpy. Please refer out
<a href="https://github.com/pgmpy/pgmpy/blob/dev/CONTRIBUTING.md">contributing guide</a>
for more details. We also offer mentorship for new contributors and maintain a
list of potential <a href="https://github.com/pgmpy/pgmpy/wiki/Mentored-Projects">mentored
projects</a>. If you are
interested in contributing to pgmpy, please join our
<a href="https://discord.gg/DRkdKaumBs" rel="nofollow">discord</a> server and introduce yourself. We will
be happy to help you get started.</p>
